| D229 | **The VSCode extension, and `calu ide`, the bridge every IDE plugin uses** (2026-11-18, requirements 23 and 34; the owner asked for it to be built now, which sets aside the order of phase 9). The specification is `agent/records/notes/vscode-extension-spec.md`. Decisions: (1) **Python first.** The extension does not speak the WebSocket protocol itself, as D200 (a) had it: it starts `calu ide`, a new command that speaks JSON-RPC over stdin/stdout, finds and attaches to sessions by the rules of `calu attach` (token-free socket, shared login, minted token), reconnects, relays the session protocol, and answers what that protocol lacks -- settings, checker instructions with their versions, model presets and "one model for every role", listing and requesting sessions, the environment check. A JetBrains plugin will start the same command. This also answers D200 (c): in a remote mode the extension host and `calu ide` run on the remote side, next to the session. (2) **Settings go through `calu ide`, not through the session** (D200 (d) stays): with the credential rules of the command line and no prompt; a credential that needs a password is asked for in VSCode and passed per request. (3) **The extension never starts `calu run` or `calu serve` as a child process**: sudo and passwords belong in a terminal, so it opens an integrated terminal with the command and then requests sessions (`session_requests`). (4) **It installs uv and calu** (`uv tool install`), checks for `claude` and never installs it. (5) **Model presets** come from calu (`calu.ide.presets`, built from `web.settings.MODEL_OPTIONS`), so the extension holds no model names. (6) Structure, build and tests follow stay-in-the-loop's extension (same author). |
| D230 | **What a review of the recent work found, and what was changed** (2026-11-18; one read-only reviewer on Opus, asked for inconsistencies, missing wiring and missing tests, and told to leave out nitpicks and the owner's deliberate weak-security options). **Changed:** (1) a `calu setup` without `--no-password`, after one with it, now gives the role `calu_admin` a new generated password (kept in the store only), so the login that had been published in the place file stops working; before, only the file lost the password and the role kept it, although the warning and the guide said the setup "takes that back". (2) `calu setup --no-password` always gives the role a generated password; converting a setup whose `admin` entry had a password used to publish that typed password in the world-readable place file. This corrects D228 (3), which said the typed password is kept on a conversion. (3) The help of `--no-password` names the world-readable file. (4) Commands that change settings take the place file's login before the user's own `calu_admin` entry, as the fleet commands do. (5) A token-free client is recorded as `root:<name>` or `local:<name>` for everything it does (certifications, cleared marks, changed settings), not only for escalation decisions. (6) The help of `--token` and of `attach --sudo` say what is true since D226-D228. (7) A client that is not root looks for `--insecure` sockets in both places a session may have chosen (`$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` and `/run/user/<uid>`). (8) A database test runs the three setups in a row (with a password, `--no-password`, without it again) and checks the file and which passwords log in. (9) **A blank password is a password** (the owner, the same day: the client must not decide that it is invalid). The password prompt of the command line, the credential store (`save`), the full-screen client's admin access, `is_admin_password` and the web login no longer refuse a blank answer themselves: it is passed to the store or to the database, which say whether it is right (PostgreSQL's "no password supplied" is reported as a wrong password). A store entry may be encrypted with a blank password. `enable_admin_login` still refuses an empty password, because PostgreSQL does not let a role log in with one. **Not changed:** `calu attach --sudo` with only a password-protected `admin` entry in root's store may ask for the admin password when a setting is saved, although the manual checklist item 31 says it asks nothing; not traced. |
| D231 | **Everything the owner asked of the IDE plugins on 2026-11-18, in one place, with its state** (requirements 34 and 35; his words are in `REQUIREMENTS.md`, the contract is `agent/records/notes/vscode-extension-spec.md` sections 1-8, the PyCharm research is `agent/records/notes/pycharm-plugin-research.md`). **VSCode extension (`extensions/vscode/`), required:** (1) modelled on stay-in-the-loop's extension, but with a better UX, dark theme first; (2) parity with Claude Code's own chat before anything else: inline images, undo (rewind to before a message), fork, several chat sessions, a settings dialog, slash commands, model and thinking-level choice, context usage always visible (percent or tokens), questions and escalations shown and handled, notifications; (3) calu's features on top, the common ones one click away: the checker context, the checker instructions (editable), honesty checking (switching the mode), all of calu's settings in a structured, searchable dialog; (4) subagents fully supported (a tab each, task and result, messages to background subagents); (5) one model for every role in one step, with presets for Anthropic and Bedrock Haiku 5.5 and Sonnet 5.5 and the other default models (the list comes from `calu.ide.presets`); (6) it installs uv and calu, and checks for `claude` without installing it; (7) switching between the sessions running on the machine with a dropdown, and names for sessions (migration 0036); (8) it runs when VSCode is connected to a remote machine, everything then runs there (`extensionKind: workspace`); (9) with no `calu serve` running it shows how to start one persistently in tmux or screen; (10) at most one password, once, at startup (`ide.unlock`: the bridge keeps the logins in memory; optional "remember" in VSCode's secret storage); nothing is asked after `calu setup --no-password`; (11) a blank password is never refused by a client. (13) direct manipulation in the transcript (spec section 11): a click on an edit's row opens the diff of that edit, a click on the file name opens the file at the edited section, and likewise for reads, paths, checker decisions and subagent launches; (14) one password per IDE start is acceptable, kept in memory by the client, never asked more often (spec section 10); (12) the plugins may assume a `calu serve` backend, and nothing on the client side needs sudo (spec section 9): the extension and `calu ide` run as the user, sessions are requested from the server, and starting the server or installing calu for root is a command a person runs in a terminal. **Architecture (D229):** the extension is thin and starts `calu ide`, the JSON-RPC bridge over stdio, which every IDE plugin uses. **State on 2026-11-18, end of the session:** committed, not pushed: the bridge with its tests (`src/calu/ide/`), the extension's host side with 147 unit tests (bridge supervision, sessions, checker file system, context document, diff, comments, install, settings panel host, shared reducer and message contracts). In work by agents when the session ended, not committed: the chat webview (`webview/main.ts`, `media/chat.css`) with the host additions of spec sections 7 and 8 (rewind, fork, slash commands, honesty menu, notifications, the one-password flow), the settings dialog (`webview/settings.ts`, `media/settings.css`), and `ide.unlock` in the bridge. **Not started:** integration tests in a real VSCode; a first run of the extension in VSCode at all; packaging (`npm run package`); user documentation (`DOCUMENTATION.md`, `README.md`, `docs/TESTING.md`). **Testing the plugins:** the owner approved Cua (`trycua/cua`): a desktop in a Docker container (`calu-uitest`, files under `uitest/`) with VSCode, PyCharm and uv installed, driven by scripts (screenshot, click, type) so that an agent can look at the UI; being set up when the session ended. The scripted tests (vitest, later `@vscode/test-cli` and Playwright against openvscode-server) stay the regression suite. **PyCharm plugin (requirement 35), after the VSCode extension:** more powerful: local mode, JetBrains remote development, and an SSH-only mode in which PyCharm works on a local copy while calu, the database and Claude Code run on a server reached over SSH, with changes synchronised both ways. Research result, nothing decided: one Kotlin plugin that starts the same `calu ide` three ways (Eel or `wsl.exe` locally, as a process on the remote backend, `ssh -T host calu ide`); file sync through the bridge itself over that SSH connection (`ide.fs.*`, to be added), Mutagen as the fallback; the VSCode webviews reused in JCEF where that works, which needs a spike for remote development; Kilo Code's plugin (MIT) as the main reference. The same one-password rule applies (same bridge; the owner was asked and has not answered). Cheap interim step: `calu acp` already starts in the owner's PyCharm through `wsl.exe` and is then refused because it answers `session/new` with the same session id each time; fix that. |
| D232 | **Session traces in the project: the chat as a reviewable, versioned file -- built** (2026-11-18, requirement 36; the owner: "chat sessions are a form of source code"; code in `calu.traces`, user documentation in `DOCUMENTATION.md`, "Session traces"). **How the build differs from the plan below.** (1) No setting `traces.redact_what`: what the redaction agent replaces is fixed in its built-in instructions (`defaults/checkers/system/trace-redaction.md`); a setting follows when somebody needs another list (superseded by D245: the instructions are the setting `traces.redactor_instructions`). (2) Further settings: `traces.redactor_timeout_s` (60), `traces.max_input_chars` (20,000, per value of a tool call's input), `traces.max_result_chars` (4,000) and `traces.queue_blocks` (500). (3) A full queue does not make a step wait: further blocks are left out and a line in the file says how many; the text of a block that was left out or withheld is never written. (4) `narrated` needs the narrator: with `context.narrate` off the file holds the plain form and its header says why. The narrations are the ones the session computes for its checkers; text the session narrates for no checker (a subagent's last words, text before an interrupt) is narrated by the trace writer's own call of the same narrator. (5) The guards apply while `traces.enabled` is on; with tracing off the directory is an ordinary directory. Reading a trace is an ordinary read for Claude; only the checkers' code tools refuse it. (6) The header of a resumed or forked session names the earlier file when a file below the trace directory names that Claude session (the 500 newest files are searched); otherwise it names the Claude session id. (7) Who decided an escalation is taken from the escalation's row (`resolved_by`) and handed to the writer beside the `escalation.resolved` event; the event itself is unchanged. (8) Stage 1 also replaces the values of environment variables whose names say that they hold a credential, and the password of the session's database login. **The plan.** **What it is.** With a setting on, calu appends every step of a session as Markdown to a file inside the project, so that the conversation can be committed and reviewed with the code it produced. Nothing is folded or summarised: the file only grows, one block per step. **Settings** (a new section `traces`, off by default): `traces.enabled`; `traces.content` = `narrated` (Claude's text and thinking in indirect speech, as the checkers read them) or `plain` (Claude's text as written; thinking left out or summarised, to be decided); `traces.directory` (default `.agent/traces`); `traces.redact` (default on) and `traces.redactor_model` (a role of its own, so "one model for every role" covers it); `traces.redact_what` (default: secrets and personal data). **Where.** `<directory>/<date>/<user>/session_NNN.md`: the date the session started (UTC, `YYYY-MM-DD`), the OS user Claude Code runs as, NNN the next free number in that directory (three digits, created with an exclusive open so that two sessions never share a file). A resumed or forked conversation is a new calu session and gets a new file whose header names the file it continues. **What a file holds.** A header (calu session id, Claude session id, project, start time, model, calu version, content mode, whether it was redacted and by which model), then per step in order: the user's message; Claude's text; each tool call with its input in a code fence and a bounded result; the checkers' decision and reason for it; escalations with who decided and the note; questions and their answers; subagents as nested sections under the step that started them; the end of each turn. No images: a line says how many were attached. No chat summary and no folded context. **Redaction.** Every block passes two stages before it reaches the disk, and nothing unredacted is ever written: (1) a deterministic pass that replaces the values calu knows to be secret (the values of the hidden environment variables and provider keys, client tokens, connection strings) and common key and token shapes; (2) the redaction agent, a model call per block (or per small batch) with fixed instructions to replace secrets and personal data (names of private persons, e-mail addresses, phone numbers, postal addresses, account and card numbers and the like) by typed placeholders such as `[redacted: api key]`, and to return the text otherwise unchanged. If the agent fails or times out, the block is written as a placeholder that says a step was withheld, and the session goes on; a failure never writes the raw text and never stops Claude. With `traces.redact` off, only stage (1) runs, and the header says so. **How it runs.** A writer of the session process, fed from the same events the store gets, with a bounded queue so that redaction never delays a step; order is kept; the queue is flushed when the session ends. calu writes the file itself, with the file access of the session's user (D206), so the files belong to that user. **Guarding.** The trace directory is calu's own output inside the project: Claude's steps that would write there are not ordinary edits (a built-in rule makes them wait for a person), the files are left out of the write sets and the honesty state, and the checkers' context does not read them back. **Decided with the build** (the lead's defaults of 2026-11-18, for the owner to confirm): in `plain` mode Claude's thinking is left out; the checkers' full context of an escalation is not in the trace (the decision, the reason, who decided and the note are); redaction runs per appended block, up to four blocks at a time, written in order; no export of past sessions in this version. **Not decided:** whether a session may be traced after the fact from the database (`calu trace export`), which the stored history would allow for the narrated form; a `.gitattributes`/review tooling for the files; batching several blocks into one call of the redaction agent (cost against delay). calu never commits them: versioning is the user's step. |
| D233 | **The test campaign of 2026-11-18/19, what it changed, and what waits for the owner** (requirements 34-38; the record of the cases is `agent/records/notes/test-plan.md`, the method for the IDE tests is the skill `.claude/skills/ui-testing-with-cua/SKILL.md`). **How it is tested.** Every front end is used by an agent as a person would use it, against a scripted calu server (`uitest/fake_server.py`: the real session machinery, a scripted runner, fake checkers, selectable scenarios) and, where the owner allowed it, a real Claude Code on Haiku with his budget-limited API key: the IDE plugins in the Cua desktop container `calu-uitest`, the web interface in a headless Chromium, the terminal clients in a private tmux server, the server and setup side in a container with root, sudo and several users (`uitest/rootbox/`), the PyCharm plugin's SSH mode against a headless container with sshd and key login (planned). Each environment has a PostgreSQL of its own. **Decisions taken on the way.** (1) The VSCode extension: the chat sits in the secondary side bar (VSCode 1.106 or newer); a new session starts at once and asks neither a first message nor a name; keyboard focus never lands on Allow by itself and Enter in a note never decides (found in the VSCode chat, then again on three web pages); inherited turns of a forked or rewound session are shown from `session.history`; a message to a subagent is shown with the user's own words (`user.input.to`); bidirectional control characters are shown as placeholders in every front end; Claude Code's own slash commands run locally and are shown as such, `/help` is not offered; a fork further back than the newest turn is retried without Claude Code's guard; a person's note on a decision and the answer to a question are user input for the checkers. (2) **Checker instructions (requirement 38):** the command, edit, changeset and subagent-result checkers got short instructions (310-355 words composed, from 640-2000) that leave general safety to Claude Code's auto mode and hold the project's own rules plus a block of test triggers: `calu:test-deny` and `calu:test-escalate` count anywhere in the step; `calu:test-allow` and `calu:test-pass` count only in a message the user typed (measured on Haiku: honoured 41 of 41 times there, 0 of 55 anywhere else). The narrator and the trace redaction are untouched. **D224 is built:** honesty is judged by the honesty checker alone; `check.result` carries its per-file result (`honesty`, `honesty_escalation`), which no client renders yet. The honesty checker's and reviewer's instructions were rewritten and measured (`agent/records/notes/honesty-evals-2026-11-18.md`: Haiku 52 of 53 edits, from 42; Sonnet is refused by the provider on the backdoor cases). (3) **Session traces** (D232) are built and were correct with a real redaction model. (4) **File sync through the bridge** for the PyCharm plugin's SSH mode is built on the Python side (`ide.fs.*`, `calu ide --cwd`, `files.user_edit` so that the user's uploads are not counted as a command's writes). (5) The PyCharm plugin targets only the latest stable PyCharm (2026.3) and, if it costs nothing, the latest EAP. (6) The test suite: unit layer in parallel (30 s), a marker `slow`, a faster test database. **Waiting for the owner's decision:** (a) auto mode is not on by default, and with the short instructions a fresh setup has no general safety judgement; a start-up warning says so; make `claude.require_auto_mode` or `claude.permission_mode = auto` the default? (b) while a decision is open, calu holds every other checked step of every agent, and a step held for 300 s raises its own decision (one unanswered decision with ten subagents became ten cards): keep, or hold only the agent concerned? (c) session traces: thinking in plain mode, redaction per step or per turn, an export of past sessions; (d) the PyCharm plugin: start the bridge at project open or with the tool window, the password over the IDE's link in remote mode, hold or upload a local save while Claude edits the file, the plugin id `io.github.kadeng.calu`; (e) after a denied commit the corrected file counts as unreviewed until it is staged, and a mark cleared by hand is not stored. |
| D234 | **calu run for real as root, through sudo and as other users: what the root box showed, and what was changed** (2026-11-18, requirement 37; the cases are `agent/records/notes/test-plan.md` section E, the environment is `uitest/rootbox`, the lasting tests are `tests/root/`). The paths of D206-D228 had run only with the tests' own uid standing in for root. They were run in a container with root, a user whose sudo asks nothing, one whose sudo asks its password and one without sudo, against PostgreSQL 16 as an existing server, with scripted sessions through calu's own command line (`fakecalu`) and a stand-in `claude` that records who started it. **Held:** the launcher (uid, gid, groups, home and an environment without keys, sudo's or calu's variables, for a name and for a bare uid), the file identity (files a session writes belong to the user; `/etc/calu`, the log and `/run/calu` are closed to the user and to a third user), the root socket and the `--insecure` socket (modes, owners, a directory planted by another user is refused), the place file and the three setups in a row, `--sudo` through a real sudo, the request life cycle of `calu serve` (limits, idle end, a claimed request whose server died), and one calu password per command. **Changed, each with a test:** (1) *Security:* `calu run --user root` and `calu serve --user root` bound the token-free root socket of D215 although Claude Code is root there too, so its own commands were root clients and could decide their own escalations, against D226 (1). The socket is bound only when Claude Code runs as another user (`RunOptions.run_as`). (2) `calu run` ended at once on SIGTERM, leaving the row in its last state, the escalation open and the socket behind; it stops as on Ctrl-C. (3) One password per command: a client with a password-protected credential was asked again at its end, to revoke its token. A root client keeps the login in memory for that (it is out of Claude Code's reach); any other client leaves the token and says so, since only that client knew it and it ends with its session. Keeping the login in a client that runs as Claude Code's own user was not chosen: it would hold the operator or owner login for hours in a process Claude's programs can read. (4) Ctrl-C and Ctrl-D at a password prompt end every command with one line. (5) Messages: a wrong session password ("calu: session"), a password the database refuses (libpq's text twice), the refusal of `--sudo` (cut before the install command), a blank admin password ("none was typed"), `calu attach` without a session (names `calu start`). (6) A refused login raised AttributeError on a Python without `os.memfd_create` (the 3.14.0 uv installs); the system call is used directly there. (7) `calu serve --sudo` opened a log file in the user's home before it elevated. (8) A stored login to a server on this machine was read as the Docker database when it did not answer and docker was missing. (9) A lost database is reported once per reason on the terminal, not once per attempt. (10) The launcher leaves out variables calu emptied to hide them. (11) `calu doctor --user` and `--sudo` (as root the file-reading part runs first, then `asuser.enter`, so Claude Code's settings and the sandbox are judged as that user; nothing is created); a store only root reads is no finding; a password in the place file and a bubblewrap that cannot create namespaces are. (12) `calu setup --set-passwords`: the way back from `--no-password` did not exist (a setup without the option un-publishes the login but leaves the stored logins without a password and the role with a password nobody knows); the option encrypts the stored logins with typed passwords and gives the role the admin password, and also changes existing passwords. **Not changed, for the owner:** `calu config`, `calu checker` and `calu rules` use the `admin` entry of the user's own store before the `operator` entry, so in an account whose two entries name different databases (the owner's own, as of 2026-11-18) they change the other database and do not name it; every `calu setup` in a store that holds the generated `calu_admin` login gives the role a new password, which a long-running `calu web` or editor bridge notices only when its connection drops; under `--sudo` Claude Code gets sudo's `PATH`; a failed session start is three lines on stderr and advises `claude` in a terminal where `calu login --user` is meant. **Not run:** Claude Code's sandbox under the other uid (the container may not create user namespaces), the Docker variant of the database, the full-screen client as root, a real conversation. |
| D235 | **The open defects of the test campaign, fixed** (2026-11-19; each with tests, the cases in `agent/records/notes/test-plan.md`). (1) **Held steps are held again.** After the last holding decision all held steps are checked at once; when one of them escalated, the approvals of the others that were being recorded became escalations of their own, depending on the order in which the checks finished. An approval that meets an open escalation at the last look now waits (`Session._held_late`, `StepGate.wait_again`), for what is left of the step's 300 s, and is looked at again; the call does not count as running and its snapshot is taken again after the wait. An error in that wait ends in ESCALATE. Which steps an open decision holds (every agent, or only the one concerned) is unchanged and still the owner's to decide (D233). (2) **A settings command says which database it changes.** `calu config`, `checker`, `rules`, `project`, `summary`, `honesty` and `web` choose: the login of the place file, a password-free `calu_admin` entry, the one stored entry of `admin` and `operator`; with both stored and naming different databases (`Target.place`) nothing is guessed and `--database admin\|operator` is required. Before a change one line goes to stderr: `changing settings in <host:port/dbname> as <role>`. Both entries are read (two passwords) only in the case of two stored entries without a choice. (3) **Start-up.** `calu.cli` no longer imports Pydantic AI (with the Mistral and Bedrock modules), the Agent SDK and `mcp` before a command needs them: `calu.checkers`, `calu.context` and `calu.evals` import their agent modules at the first access to one of their names (`calu.lazy`), `calu.run` imports the session and the SDK runner where a session starts, `calu.claude_model` and `calu.models` import a provider where a model is built, the bundled `claude` is located without importing the SDK. `calu --version` 1.9 s to 0.5 s; `tests/unit/test_startup_imports.py` holds it. (4) **Session traces.** A changed `traces` setting starts a new file only if it changes where the file is or what a block holds (`queue_blocks` and `redactor_timeout_s` count from the next file on); an idle session switches with its next block; a file that got no block of the session is removed when it is closed. (5) **A revoked token and its request.** The connection of a revoked token was closed 0.1 s after the database's answer, so that the -32001 response of the request that noticed it would go out first; on a busy machine the close came first and the client got a closed connection (code 4001) instead of the response. The close now waits for that response (`Client.revoke`, at most 2 s). The request was not served in either order. (6) **Honesty.** A file whose working content is verified while the index holds an earlier, unverified content (`Unreviewed.restage`) no longer asks a person: the step is denied with the `git add` that settles it, and the denial of a commit for a mark names `git add` in advance. Clearing the last open mark of a file by hand records the file's content as trusted with the source `user`, the record `/trust-file` makes, so a later session does not mark the same content again (before: the clearing held for the session only). (7) **Smaller ones.** `calu web` after `--no-password` no longer advises `calu setup`; a `calu web` whose settings login was replaced says so and to start it again; `/history` shows each turn's input as a heading and marks failed calls; a turn's row is written when the turn starts and again when it ends (a history holds finished turns only), so a conversation stopped in its first turn is listed; `calu resolve` records `operator:<os user>@<host>` (migration 0037: the role is what the database checked, user and host are what the caller says); a conversation whose first message went to a subagent is listed with the user's words (`calu.context.foreign.own_words`); the browser layer is `-m browser` in the documents, as in CI. |
| D236 | **The PyCharm plugin's SSH mode, built and tested against a real SSH server** (2026-11-19; requirement 35: "the older local IDE with SSH sync and remote execution via SSH mode"). The plugin starts `ssh -T -o BatchMode=yes ... <host> calu ide --cwd <dir>` with the system's OpenSSH client and synchronises the local project with the remote one through the bridge's file requests (`ide.fs.*`, D229 and `agent/records/notes/jetbrains-sync-spec.md` sections 13-14): clone into an empty folder after a confirmation, both directions as files change, a local save held while Claude edits the same file in a turn and merged afterwards, a conflict resolved in the IDE's three-way merge dialog (base = last synced content, kept in the plugin's state directory, not in the project), an offline queue replayed after a reconnect, links never followed, special and over-limit files skipped with a note, `.gitignore` honoured on the local side by the plugin itself (the clone has no `.git`). Writes go through the IDE's `Document` when the file is open (undoable). Measured against the test server `uitest/sshbox/` (headless Ubuntu, key login only, a key pair made for the tests): clone of 307 files 1.3 s; Claude's edit in the local editor 0.3-0.8 s; a local save on the server about 0.2 s; the bridge's polling watcher 0.6 % of a core. The setting "Sync: none" leaves synchronisation to the user (PyCharm's Deployment, rsync, Mutagen). **Not verified:** two IDE windows on one server project, "Sync: none" by hand, a confirmed message to a subagent over SSH, the installer run. **Found for the core** (not changed by this round): `session.history` omits a turn that has not ended; the VSCode and terminal clients tell replayed turns by their text instead of the prompt uuid; scripted tool ids repeat across sessions; a dead session stays listed as running for minutes; `ide.fs.changed` does not name skipped files. **Rough edges:** PyCharm writes a sample `main.py` into an empty project folder, so a clone becomes a plan with one upload; a calu installed with `uv tool install` is not on the PATH of a non-interactive SSH command (link it into `/usr/local/bin` or set "calu command"); no conflict marks in the project view; no percentage in the clone's progress (the platform's API for it is internal). |
| D237 | **The core defects of the SSH round and the open ones of D235, fixed** (2026-11-19; each with a test that fails before). (1) **A turn that has not ended is history.** A turn's row is stored at its start (input and prompt uuid) and again at its end; `session.history` and the context a resume or fork reads left a row without end, steps and answer out, so a fork made while a turn waited on a decision lacked a prompt that Claude Code's copy of the transcript holds. Every stored turn is now returned (`ended_at` None for one that has not ended: the running turn, a turn cut off with its session). A fork copies such a turn with its inputs and prompt uuid, no steps and no answer, and ends it at the fork's start: the fork never continues it, and its steps stay with the session that ran them (D65). A client tells the running turn of a live session from the replayed events by the prompt uuid; the web page marks it "not ended". (2) **Record and replay meet at the prompt uuid.** The VSCode extension (`shared/history.ts`) and the terminal client (`SessionModel.load_history`) found the boundary by the text of a message, so equal words in two turns dropped the turns between them. The prompt uuid decides where the turn has one and the replayed inputs have any; the text only otherwise (the PyCharm plugin's rule since 0b1cab1). (3) The scripted runner's tool and message ids carry a random prefix per runner, so they no longer repeat across sessions. (4) **Stale sooner.** The heartbeat is 10 s and a session is stale after 60 s of silence (was 30 s and 180 s), and at once when the reader runs in the process table the session recorded (`sessions.machine`) and no process has its pid, or the session ran on this machine before its last reboot (`herd.process_gone`, `SessionRow.gone`): a restarted `calu serve` no longer leaves its dead sessions listed as running. A session in a container that was stopped is judged by its silence (another pid namespace cannot be judged from here). (5) **`ide.fs.changed` names skipped paths.** A notification carries `skipped: [{path, reason}]` (the manifest's shape) for each path that became one the bridge does not synchronise (above the size limit, a special file, a link); a regular file that becomes skipped is not reported as deleted. The PyCharm plugin lists such a path at once and keeps its local copy (`Synchronizer.remoteChanged`). (6) **Protocol event `step.held`** `{tool_use_id, agent_id, tool_name, held, reason}`: sent when a hold begins and when it ends, from `EscalationDesk.hold`, both before a step's check and after its approval was recorded (D235 (1)). The full-screen client shows `held` from it also for a step whose check is known (it showed ALLOW); the plain terminal and ACP leave it out (the log line says the same); the web page reads the hold from that log line as before; VSCode and PyCharm show no held steps and ignore the event. (7) A decision in the web interface (escalation page and triage) records `<role>:<os user>@<host>` of `calu web`, as `calu resolve` does (schema 37). (8) `calu ide`: `ide.start.cancel` no longer aborts a start whose outcome the server has reported: the start leaves the list of cancellable waits in the step in which `wait_for` returns. Also: the `calu.cli` contracts of HANDOFF item 2 (`_local_socket_endpoint`, the login order of `_herd_command` and `_herd_value`, what they keep, `token_id` None on the socket paths, `_admin_secret`'s `_typed`, `_admin_command`'s `Raises`) and tests for `ide.models.set` with `checker:<name>`, the escalations `ide.status`, `ide.sessions` and `ide.attach` close, and `ide.start`'s `SERVER_REFUSED` and unnamed paths. |
| D238 | **The system of records moved to `agent/records/`; session traces default to `agent/traces/`** (2026-11-19; requirement 36, the owner's addendum). `HANDOFF.md`, `PLAN.md`, `REQUIREMENTS.md` and `docs/notes/` (now `agent/records/notes/`) moved with `git mv`; `AGENTS.md` stays at the top level and explains `agent/`. Every reference was rewritten: paths in documents, tests, CI, scripts, Kotlin and TypeScript, and the `PLAN.md D…` citations in code comments and docstrings, which now read `agent/records/PLAN.md D…` (a docstring summary that grew past 100 columns got its citation as a `See …` paragraph). Inside the three top-level records, sibling names stay bare. The parent project's `docs/PLAN.md` and test fixtures that name a `docs/PLAN.md` are not references and were left alone. The traces' default directory is `agent/traces` (`TracesSettings.directory`; also never synchronised by `calu ide`, `TRACE_DIRECTORY`, and by the PyCharm plugin, `PathMapper.DEFAULT_EXCLUDED`); a project that set `traces.directory` keeps its own. `web/settings.REFERENCE` accepts both pointer forms. |
| D239 | **`calu setup` asks about the sandbox; with it off, a recommendation about the logins** (2026-11-19, requirement 42). (1) **The question.** Every `calu setup` asks whether Claude Code's sandbox is on (`claude.sandbox.enabled` in the global settings), after two lines that say what it does and whether `bubblewrap` and `socat` are installed (with `sudo apt install bubblewrap socat` when not). Default: the stored value; with none stored, on when both programs are installed and off otherwise. `--sandbox`/`--no-sandbox` answer it for a script. Without a terminal and without the option nothing is written and the report names the value and where it comes from: a setup never changes the setting unasked. The answer is written only when it differs from the stored value or none is stored. (2) **The recommendation.** With the sandbox off: "it is strongly recommended to at least protect the session and database logins with passwords and/or run calu with --sudo, so that calu runs as a more privileged process than Claude Code" (`preflight.SANDBOX_OFF_ADVICE`), at setup (stderr), among every session's start-up warnings (`calu run`, the sessions of `calu serve`) and as a `calu doctor --db` note. It is left out only when it is known to hold (`preflight.LoginProtection.met`): calu runs as root and Claude Code as another user (`--sudo`/`--user`; at setup: setup runs as root), or every entry of the credential store has a password; in both cases the place file must publish no password of `calu_admin` (`sudo calu setup --no-password` puts one there, readable by every user). The facts are collected by the command before the switch to the user (`RunOptions.login_protection`, `ServeOptions.login_protection`); a session embedded without them warns. A blank password counts as a password (D230 (9)). `calu doctor` reads the setting only with `--db`, from the global settings; a project's own value is not looked at there, while a session judges its effective configuration. (3) **No refusal without the sandbox.** Measured (`spikes/RESULTS.md`, 2026-11-19): Claude Code 2.1.319 starts with `enabled: false, failIfUnavailable: true` and neither `bwrap` nor `socat` on `PATH`, and refuses with `enabled: true`; calu's own checks refuse nothing while the sandbox is off (`settings_file_sandbox_blockers` returns none), so nothing changed there. (4) **Not built:** a check that a typed password is strong; the session-start warning does not know about `calu credential set ... --no-password` entries of another machine. |
| D240 | **The root-only tests that were missing, and what they found** (2026-11-19; the rest of the coverage round, run as real root in the root box, `tests/root/`). (1) **Fixed: `asuser.prepare` followed a symbolic link** in the place of the state home (`/var/lib/calu/state/<uid>`) or of a bare uid's home (`/var/lib/calu/claude/<uid>`) and gave its target to the user (owner, mode 0700). Only a writer of `state/` or `claude/` could plant such a link, which are root's, so this is defence in depth; it now opens the directory without following a link (`O_NOFOLLOW`, `O_DIRECTORY`), sets owner and mode on that descriptor and refuses anything else (`UserError`). (2) **Held, now tested:** `prepare` on existing directories (a root-owned 0755 state home and a third user's 0777 home become the user's, 0700, their content and its owners kept; a named user's home is not touched); the launcher as root refuses a record whose uid, gid or group the system calls read as "unchanged" (-1 and 2^32-1), so Claude Code never starts as root or with root's group; `cli._after_setup` writes `/var/lib/calu/database.json` root's, 0644, trusted by another user, the owner's password never, the generated `calu_admin` password only with `--no-password` and gone again after a setup with a typed password; `calu serve --user UID` runs Claude Code (a stand-in that answers the SDK's start-up requests) with the uid and gid on all three slots, no groups, the bare uid's home, in the project, and binds the session's root socket 0600, removed after SIGTERM; the sandbox-off advice of D239 is not shown by a `--user` session or by `sudo calu setup` with passwords, and is shown by both, with its reason, after `sudo calu setup --no-password`. |
| D241 | **Auto mode by default; thinking in the plain session trace** (2026-11-19, the owner's answers of the afternoon; requirement 36). (1) **What a session got.** Measured with CLI 2.1.319 and claude-agent-sdk 0.2.179 without a model call (the API base URL pointed at a closed local port, so only the init message arrived): with no settings file that sets `permissions.defaultMode`, `claude -p --input-format stream-json` and a `ClaudeSDKClient` without `permission_mode` both report `permissionMode: "default"` in the init message, with and without the variables of a surrounding Claude Code session; an interactive `claude` in a pseudo-terminal under the same empty home shows "Auto mode is now Claude Code's default permission mode" and "auto mode on"; the SDK passes `--permission-mode` only when it is set. With `"defaultMode": "auto"` in `~/.claude/settings.json` (as on the owner's machine) the SDK start reports "auto". (2) **Built.** `claude.permission_mode` unset now means the mode a plain `claude` starts in: calu passes `--permission-mode auto` unless a loaded settings file sets `permissions.defaultMode`, which Claude Code then applies (`preflight.launch_permission_mode`); a value of `claude.permission_mode` is passed as it is. Another mode is the user's choice: the start-up warnings about it (`permission_mode_warnings`, and `thin_checker_warnings` for the short default instructions without auto mode) are removed; the runner logs the effective mode as information, and as a warning only when it started Claude Code with "auto" and the main agent reports another mode (a model without auto mode); `calu doctor`'s line is "permission mode", always ok, naming the mode and its source. `claude.require_auto_mode` is unchanged. (3) **Thinking in the plain trace.** The renderer keeps one pending thinking block per agent: streamed fragments (`assistant.delta` with `thinking`) and completed texts (`assistant.thinking`) of the same message id, or a completed text after fragments only, replace it; it is written once, as its last completed text (else the fragments), when the agent's text, tool call, check or escalation comes, its thinking of another message begins, the subagent stops, or the turn or the trace ends. Each thinking block is redacted on its own, like every block. The narrated form is unchanged (thinking goes to the narrator with the text). The scripted runner gained `message` and `stream` for thinking actions. **Not done:** a rewrite in the narrated form appends the second version to the narrator's input as well; the behaviour of a real summarized-thinking rewrite (which events, which ids) was not measured here. |
| D242 | **One relay port per `calu serve` for all its sessions, with a token per server; a local client of a gone session ends at once** (2026-11-19, the owner's two answers of the afternoon; test plan section I, cases I17-I30). (1) **Ports.** `calu serve` listens on one relay port on 127.0.0.1 (default 8811, fixed so that a forward written once survives a restart; `--relay-port N`, 0 for any free port; `--no-relay`). If the default is taken the server takes a free port and prints it; a port named with `--relay-port` that is taken ends the server. Each session keeps its own loopback port, tokens and sockets as before (D20, D215, D227); sessions of `calu run` (no server) are unchanged and have no relay. (2) **How a client names the session: the path.** `ws://127.0.0.1:8811/` is the relay's own JSON-RPC (`initialize` with the server token, `relay.sessions`, `relay.start`; `RELAY_REQUESTS`, the `relay` part of `calu protocol`); `ws://127.0.0.1:8811/session/<session row id>` is that session's own protocol, unchanged, with the server token in `initialize`. A path rather than an `initialize` parameter, because every client already takes an address and a token: an address on the relay is a session address like any other, and the existing clients (TUI, line client, `calu web`, `calu acp`, `calu ide`) need no other change to attach. A client tells the relay from a session by the `relay` key of the `initialize` result. (3) **The server token.** Table `servers` (migration 0038): one row per `calu serve` process with host, OS user, pid, `machine` and relay address, written through `calu_server_started` at the start and ended through `calu_server_ended`, which also revokes its tokens. `server_tokens` holds only SHA-256, label, last use and `revoked`. Minted with the operator login by `calu token mint --server [SERVER ID]` (default: the running server of this host and OS user, `herd.process_gone` filtering dead rows), printed once with the `ssh -N -L` and `calu attach` lines; listed by `calu token list` below the client tokens; revoked by `calu token revoke --server ID`. The session role checks a hash through `calu_server_token_ok(server, hash)` (SECURITY DEFINER; returns the label or NULL, stamps `last_used_at`) and cannot read hashes. Valid until revoked or until its server ends: a restarted server needs a new token, as a client token ends with its session. (4) **What it authorises.** Attaching to every session of that one server and everything a session's client token allows there (messages, decisions, interrupt, `shutdown` of a session); listing the sessions; and asking that server for a new, resumed, forked or rewound session (`relay.start`). The last was allowed after weighing D208: whoever holds the token can already steer every session of that server, whose Claude Code runs as the same user with the same reach, so a new session adds no right; D208's point, that Claude Code must not be able to ask for sessions, holds because it has neither a database login nor the token (printed once, stored hashed, never written to a file on the server). The request is still a `session_requests` row, filed by `calu_relay_session_request(server, hash, ...)`, which checks the token and writes `requested_by = relay` and the token's label, so the claim and finish path of D208 is unchanged and the table says who asked. A session login without a valid server token still cannot write a request (test). (5) **`--secure` and `--insecure`.** `--insecure` lets a loopback peer use the relay without a token to attach (as D227 does for the sessions; a forwarded port arrives on loopback, documented), but `relay.start` always needs a server token, so that without one only an operator login can start sessions. `--secure` changes nothing for the relay: it never trusts a peer without a token, and binds no root socket. (6) **How it forwards.** In the same process: for `/session/<id>` the relay checks the server token, opens its own WebSocket connection to the session's own address and sends `initialize` with the session's own client token (the one `calu.run` makes for each session, handed to the server in memory through `on_serving` and never printed under `calu serve`) and the client's name extended by `via relay (<label>)`, then copies every message unchanged in both directions. The session's own token check runs on that connection as on any other, so the relay adds a check and removes none; a decision is recorded under the session's token with the client name (`client:calu attach via relay (laptop)`). The relay checks the server token again before every request not in `READ_ONLY_METHODS` and every 15 s, and closes a connection of a revoked token with 4003 (`CLOSE_SERVER_TOKEN_REVOKED`); the session's close code and reason are passed on (1011 for one that cannot be sent). (7) **Clients.** `calu attach ws://127.0.0.1:8811 [--session ID]`: one session attaches; several are listed and chosen on a terminal (number or id prefix), else named with `--session`; none names `calu start DIR --relay ADDRESS`, which asks the server through the relay (DIR is a path on the server). The full-screen client gets `RelayAdmin`: `/running`, `/switch`, `/new`, `/fork`, `/rewind` through the relay (`AdminAccess.sessions_problem` and `session_host` were added for that); saving instructions, `/honesty <mode>` and `/rename` say they need the server's machine. The line client has `/running`, `/switch`, `/new`, `/fork` through a relay. `calu web` falls back to the session server's relay when a session's own address cannot be reached, minting a server token labelled "calu web (live view)": one forward of the relay port covers the live view of every session of that server. (8) **Reconnect (the owner's second answer).** Remote addresses keep the 5-minute retry window. A client that found its session on this machine (a database row with `pid` and `machine`, or a local socket whose listening pid the kernel reports, `localsocket.peer_pid`) asks `herd.process_gone` (resp. whether the pid exists) when the connection breaks, and ends at once with "the session ended: its process is gone" (`Link(gone=...)`, `attach(gone=...)`). Through a relay a session that has ended is answered "no session ... runs on this server" at the next attempt, which ends the client too. (9) **Tested** with a scripted session behind the relay (`tests/unit/test_relay.py`), the tunnel pattern of `tests/unit/test_remote_attach.py` extended to the relay and to the gone check, the database layer (`tests/db/test_serve.py`: the whole path with a real `calu serve`, the session login's limits, `calu token mint --server`, the web fallback), and over a real SSH forward against a copy of the SSH box (test plan I17-I30). **Not built:** a relay for `calu run`; TLS. (Listing names through the relay: D244.) |
| D243 | **`calu serve` serves one projects directory, and its relay port is derived from that directory** (2026-11-19, the owner's two decisions: requirement 43, and "Port from directory"; test plan section I, cases I31-I37). (1) **The projects directory.** `calu serve [--projects DIR]`, default the current directory, resolved with `realpath` before any check (`calu.projects_dir.check_projects_dir`). The command refuses to start, with one line and before it asks for the password, when the resolved directory is `/`, is hidden or inside a hidden directory (any component starting with "."), or lies in a system directory: `/etc`, `/proc`, `/sys`, `/dev`, `/boot`, `/bin`, `/sbin`, `/lib`, `/lib32`, `/lib64`, `/libx32`, `/run`, `/snap`, all of `/usr` (decided: `/usr/local` too, it holds installed software, and one rule is easier to state than five subdirectories), `/var/lib`, `/var/log`, `/var/cache`, `/var/spool`, `/var/mail`, `/var/run`, `/var/lock`, `/var/backups`, `/var/db`; on macOS `/System`, `/Library`, `/Applications`, `/cores`, `/private/etc` and the same directories of `/private/var` (compared without case on macOS). `/home`, `/srv`, `/opt`, `/var/www`, `/tmp` and `/mnt` stay allowed (the test suite's directories are in `/tmp`). **Root's home** (`/root`, `/var/root`) is refused unless Claude Code runs as root (calu as root with `--user root`): a root server with `--user ada` runs Claude Code as ada, who cannot use root's home, so it serves a directory of ada's; root serving its own home to a root Claude Code is the one case where it is that user's projects. (2) **Which requests it starts.** Every request the server claims, not only the relay's: the requested directory, resolved, must be the projects directory or below it, with no hidden component below it (`served_path`; a symbolic link inside that points outside is refused). Why all requests: the server's directory is what its operator put it in charge of; a `calu start`, a web-interface "new session" and the full-screen client's `/new` reach the same server, and a rule that holds for one path but not another would only move the request to the open one. A refusal names the directory served and what to run instead (`calu serve --projects <a directory that holds it>`, or `calu run` there). `relay.start` checks before it files a row (no row is written for a refused directory) and files the resolved path. `calu run` has no server and no projects directory, unchanged. (3) **Two servers of one user** (migration 0039). The `servers` row records `projects_directory`; a request filed through a relay records its `server_id`. The server claims through `calu_claim_served_request(server, pid)`: its own relay's requests, and operators' requests of its host and user whose directory is inside its projects directory or inside no other running server's (compared as text; the server then checks the resolved path). So the server that serves a directory starts it, a request outside every server's is still answered (refused with the reason, not a ten-second "no server answered"), and a relay request is never taken by another server of the same user (before, `relay.start` would have waited 120 s for a request that another server had claimed). A server whose row could not be written (schema before 0039) claims as before and still refuses outside its directory. Known limit, fixed by D244: a server that died without ending its row still counted as serving its directory, so a request there was not claimed until that row was ended. (4) **The relay port.** Default: 8811 + (SHA-256 of the resolved path as a number) mod 189, i.e. one of 8811-8999, moved on past ports other programs use (8834, 8843, 8880, 8883, 8888, 8889, 8983) (`relay_ports`). The same directory gets the same port after every restart on every machine, so a forward written once keeps working; servers of different directories mostly get different ports. When it is taken, the server says so and takes the next free port of the sequence (wrapping in the range) and prints it, rather than refusing: the owner's aim is a stable port, and the usual cause of a taken port is a second server of the same directory (or another program), for which refusing would only add a step; the printed line, the `servers` row and `calu token mint --server` carry the port actually taken. All 182 taken ends the server. `--relay-port N` still names it (taken: the server ends, as in D242; 0: any free port), `--no-relay` turns it off; a server without relay now records its row too (relay address ""), so that it claims by its directory. (5) **Shown to clients.** `RelayServer.projects_directory` in the relay's `initialize`; `calu attach` through a relay names it in its session list and in the "no session runs" hint; `calu token mint --server` prints it with the server; `ServerRow.line` (`calu token mint --server` with several servers) shows it; `calu serve` prints it in its "answering session requests" line. `uitest/fake_server.py` serves the home directory by default (`--projects`), because the test machines start it in the source tree. (6) **Tested:** `tests/unit/test_projects_dir.py` (system, hidden, root's home, symbolic links into refused directories, `/`, relative paths, the command refusing before the password, the port: stable, in range, reserved ports skipped, the sequence), `tests/unit/test_serve_unit.py` (a claimed request outside, hidden or through a link refused; `relay_start` refusing before filing; the next port when the derived one is taken; a named port taken), `tests/unit/test_relay.py` (a real `Server` behind the relay refuses `relay.start` outside, error -32000), `tests/db/test_serve.py` (the whole path with a server; which of two servers claims which request). Not run by hand over SSH yet (`docs/TESTING.md` item 39). |
| D244 | **A `calu serve` that died without ending its row stops counting as running; `/running` names the servers' projects directories** (2026-11-19; test plan I38-I41, migration 0040). (1) **The defect.** A server killed without a chance to end its `servers` row (SIGKILL, a stopped container, a machine that went away) kept counting as running: `calu_claim_served_request` left the requests in its projects directory to it, so nobody started them, and its server tokens, `calu token mint --server`, `calu web`'s relay fallback and `ide.status`'s `servers_running` treated it as alive (`herd.process_gone` filtered some of these on the operator's side, not the claim). (2) **Liveness of servers, as of sessions (D56, D237).** `servers.last_seen` (migration 0040) is stamped every `herd.HEARTBEAT_S` (10 s) by `calu_server_beat` from the server's own task (`Server._beat`) and by every claim. A server counts as running while its row has not ended and it was stamped within `herd.STALE_AFTER_S` (60 s; the SQL function `calu_server_live` uses the same 60 s). **Gone at once:** before each claim round the server asks `calu_server_peers` for the open rows of its host, OS user and recorded process table (`servers.machine` equal), looks up each pid (`herd.process_gone`) and ends a row whose process is gone through `calu_server_gone`, which revokes its tokens and refuses (false) unless both rows are open and record the same host, user and process table. The database cannot see processes, so the judgement is the calling server's, limited to a row it can judge. (3) **Where the rule is applied.** The session login cannot read `servers`, so the tests are inside the SECURITY DEFINER functions: `calu_claim_served_request` (a stale server no longer keeps another server from the requests in its directory; the claimer's own row only needs to be open, and the claim stamps it), `calu_server_token_ok` and `calu_relay_session_request` (a stale server's tokens are refused: attaching, `relay.start`). On the operator's side `servers.running` returns only running servers (row open, stamp within 60 s, process not known to be gone), which `calu token mint --server`, `ide.status`, `calu web`'s `_server_of` and the full-screen client use; `servers.mint` refuses a stale row; `ServerRow` has `silent_for_s`, `gone`, `stale`, `live`, and its line says `SILENT for N min` or `GONE`. A token is therefore refused after a minute of silence and for good once the row is ended; a stale server that beats again (a database outage, not a death) counts again. (4) **A row ended while its server runs** (an operator ended it, or a peer misjudged it): the beat finds it ended, the server records a new row, names it on stderr and gives the relay the new id; the old tokens stay revoked. (5) **The full-screen client's `/running`** lists below the sessions one line per running server, "calu serve of user@host serves DIR" (`AdminAccess.served`: `servers.running` with the admin login, the relay's `initialize` through `RelayAdmin`; read with the same password as the sessions). Through a relay it now shows the sessions' names and how long the current state has lasted: `relay.sessions` entries carry `name` and `state_since` (protocol, additive), which the relay reads through `calu_server_sessions(server, token hash, ids)` -- only for the holder of a valid token of a running server and only for sessions of that server's pid and process table; an `--insecure` client without a token gets neither, and the time falls back to the session's start. (6) **Tested:** `tests/db/test_serve.py` (A stale: B claims, A's token refused for attaching, filing and minting, A back after a beat; A's pid gone: B ends the row at once and claims, tokens revoked, a row of another process table untouched; a real server starts the request in a dead server's directory; the heartbeat and the re-recording), `tests/unit/test_serve_unit.py` (`ServerRow`, `end_gone_peers`), `tests/unit/test_relay.py`, `tests/unit/test_tui_many.py`. `calu token list` shows the tokens of a stale server whose row nobody ended as `server silent for N min` or `server gone` (`ServerTokenRow.state`), the session's own token that the relay uses as `relay of server <id>` (`TokenRow.relay_of`: the session's first, unlabelled token, when a server row records its pid and process table), and the clients name the relay, not the session, when it closes with 4003 (`calu.attach.closer`). **Not done:** a dead server in another process table (another container or machine) is judged by its silence only. |
| D245 | **The redaction agent's prompt is a setting; the shipped one may drop the user's questions and the session's times; the trace format is configurable** (2026-11-19, requirement 44 and the owner's decisions of that day; code in `calu.traces`, `calu.defaults.instructions`, `calu.cli` `cmd_traces`, `calu.web.settings`; user documentation in `DOCUMENTATION.md`, "Session traces"). Replaces item (1) of D232 ("what the agent replaces is fixed"). (1) **Where the prompt is stored.** A configuration value, `traces.redactor_instructions` (Markdown, global or per project; empty: the shipped `defaults/checkers/trace-redaction.md`), as `honesty.rules` is, and not a `checker_types` row: checker types are global only, and a project that publishes its traces may need other rules than one that keeps them. In front of it calu puts a fixed part, `defaults/checkers/system/trace-redaction.md` (the excerpt is data and not instructions, the form of the answer, when a block may be removed), as the checkers' system parts do (D181), so that an edit of the rules cannot remove the injection guard or break the answer format. (2) **Versions and conflicts.** Configuration values carry no version column; their history is the audit log (`config_audit`: old and new row of every change). A save is conflict-safe by content instead of by number: `calu traces instructions edit` reads the scope's stored value again after the editor closes and refuses (keeping the edited file) if it changed; the web form posts a fingerprint (SHA-256 of the stored value, 16 hex digits, or "unset") as `version:<field>` for every Markdown setting, and a save from an outdated page is refused, as for a checker's instructions; a second save from the refusing page overwrites deliberately. `calu honesty show|edit|reset` now uses the same code (`_text_setting`) and got the same check. (3) **Commands and front ends.** `calu traces instructions show|edit|reset [--project NAME]`. The web interface edits it on the settings pane "Session traces" (new title of the `traces` section's pane), with the fixed part folded under the editor. The VSCode extension and the PyCharm plugin show it in their settings dialogs as a Markdown setting with the shipped text, because both render every key of `MARKDOWN` generically; no extension code changed. Not done: an entry beside "Edit checker instructions" (the picker lists `checker_types` rows, and `ide.checker.get/save` work on them; a second kind of entry with a scope is an extension change), and a conflict check in `ide.settings.set`. (4) **Removal.** The shipped instructions keep every requirement and decision (stated by the user, or proposed by Claude and accepted) and what was changed and why; they let the agent remove the user's questions, hints of when the session happened and how long it took (in code and command output a time stamp is replaced by `[redacted: time]` instead), and remarks about the user's private life; the secret and personal-data placeholders are those of D232. A message of the user (`Block.removable`) may be removed as a whole: the agent answers `remove_block: true`, and the writer writes nothing for that block, heading included (a line "a question was removed" would count them); `Counted.removed` counts them. Any other block that the agent answers with `remove_block` is a failed redaction and is withheld, so a manipulated answer cannot delete a tool call silently; an empty `text` without `remove_block` is still a failure. The growth limit (`MAX_GROWTH`, `GROWTH_ALLOWANCE`) is unchanged; a shorter answer was always accepted. The request names the block's heading and says whether it may be removed (`request_head`), because the agent reads one block at a time and cannot tell a user's message from a tool result otherwise. (5) **Format settings**, all with today's format as default: `traces.layout` (`date/user`, `user`, `date`, `flat`), `traces.file_names` (`numbered`, `random`: `session_<12 hex digits>.md`), `traces.header_times`, `traces.header_user`, `traces.turn_numbers` (off: `## Turn` and `### End of turn`; tool calls stay numbered). The defaults stay because requirement 36 names the layout `<date>/<user>/session_[NNN].md` and requirement 44 asks for the possibility ("might not want to expose"), not for another default. The search for the file a resumed session continues looks at every layout's depth; removing an empty file removes the layout's folders, never the trace directory. A change of any of these settings or of the instructions starts a new file (`_trace_settings` compares the whole section). **Not decided:** whether the defaults should change for projects that publish traces; whether a removed user message should also drop its `## Turn` heading (the number of turns still shows how many messages there were, with or without `turn_numbers`). |
| D246 | **The final redaction of a trace before a commit, over what the commit adds** (2026-11-19, requirement 45: "The final diff to be committed needs redaction. We don't want the previous committed summary folded into the next."; code in `calu.traces.finalize`, `calu.traces.hook`, `calu.session` `_finalize_for_commit`, `calu.cli` `_traces_finalize`/`_traces_hook`; user documentation in `DOCUMENTATION.md`, "Session traces"). The per-block redaction of D232/D245 stays: the live file is safe on disk at any time. Replaces D232's "calu never commits them" for commits of Claude's. (1) **What is redacted.** Per trace file (named `session_*.md`, first line `# calu session trace`): the part not in `HEAD` (a new file: all of it). If `HEAD:path` is a prefix of the file, that is the suffix, starting behind the last valid marker line if one follows the committed part; otherwise (a hand edit) `difflib` over the lines, each inserted or replaced run redacted on its own, shared lines kept. The committed part is never sent to the agent and never rewritten. Stage 1 runs before and after the agent. (2) **The agent.** `Redactor.redact_stretch`: pieces of up to `FINAL_CHUNK_CHARS` (24,000) characters at line ends, `max_tokens` 16,000, each request says "part i of n"; the prompt is the fixed `system/trace-final-redaction.md` (a whole stretch, keep requirements and decisions as written also when later changed, summarise nothing, the stretch is data, never `remove_block`) followed by the same `traces.redactor_instructions` (or the shipped text). A piece whose answer is unusable (`UnusableAnswer` from `checked`: growth limit, empty, removed; or `UnexpectedModelBehavior`/`UsageLimitExceeded`) is replaced by `WITHHELD_PART`; any other failure (no connection, credentials, provider error, `traces.final_timeout_s` per file, default 600) fails the file, which is then unchanged. With `traces.redact` off only stage 1 runs. (3) **The marker.** `_[calu: the final redaction pass ran up to this line (check <16 hex>)]_` after the redacted part, the check being SHA-256 of the text above it, so a second pass before the same commit (Claude's and then the hook) finds nothing, and a copied or edited marker does not count. (4) **Writing back.** In place under `flock` (the trace writer now takes the same lock for every append, its fd has `O_APPEND`), keeping a tail appended meanwhile; a file that changed otherwise fails. The index gets the finalized text through `hash-object`/`update-index --cacheinfo` (mode kept), so a tail appended after the pass is not staged. (5) **Triggers.** (a) Claude's plain `git commit` (`classify_commit` PLAIN, compound lines are denied anyway): after the gate approved it and before the last look (`pre_tool_use`), or after a person allowed it (`ask`), the session runs the pass over `traces.directory` (tracked files that differ from `HEAD` and untracked files `.gitignore` does not exclude), writes back and stages; its own trace writer is held (`TraceWriter.hold`) until the commit's tool result, at most `final_timeout_s` + 600 s, so `git commit -a` records the same text. A failure denies the commit (source "traces", `FINAL_PASS_FAILED`, a warning in the log): a commit must not take an unfinalized trace along, and the hook path refuses as well, so the rule is the same everywhere. (b) `calu traces hook install [--chain]` writes a POSIX `pre-commit` hook (where `git rev-parse --git-path hooks/pre-commit` says, so `core.hooksPath` is followed) that runs `calu traces finalize --staged` with the absolute path of calu, falling back to `calu` on `PATH`; it refuses a hook calu did not write (recognised by `# calu-traces-hook`); `--chain` renames that one to `pre-commit.calu-chained` and runs it afterwards; `remove` restores it. Hook managers get a documented `local` hook instead. Never installed by calu itself. (c) `calu traces finalize` by hand (files on disk, not staged) and `--staged` (the index; the file on disk follows when it holds the staged text plus a tail). (6) **Without a session.** `--staged` plans first and reads settings and credentials only when a file has a new part, so a commit without one needs no login. The settings come through `_herd_value` (place-file login, password-free `calu_admin`, `operator`, `admin`), a password is asked only on a terminal; the model keys come from calu's env file. When the settings cannot be read or the agent fails, the command fails with `FINALIZE_REFUSED`, which tells the user to run `calu traces finalize --staged` in a terminal and commit again, and that `git commit --no-verify` commits the files as the per-block pass wrote them (the user's own decision; inside a session the session's pass runs regardless of `--no-verify`). **Not decided / not done:** a `git commit -a` in a session can take along the tail of another live session's trace file in the same project (redacted per block only; the hook, if installed, finalizes it); the pass's prompt is not measured on a real model; the hook inside Claude's own commit gets Claude's environment without the checker keys, which matters only when the session's pass did not run (`traces.final_redaction` off with the hook installed). |
| D247 | **Every client switches Claude Code's permission mode while a session runs** (2026-11-19, the owner's decision: Claude Code's Shift+Tab in every front end, plan mode only if trivial; measured in `spikes/spike_permission_mode_switch.py`, CLI 2.1.319, SDK 0.2.179, `spikes/RESULTS.md`). (1) **Measured.** A running Claude Code takes default, acceptEdits, plan, auto and dontAsk through `set_permission_mode`; bypassPermissions is refused ("not launched with --dangerously-skip-permissions"). After each change it sends a system message `status` with `permissionMode`; a change while a call waits in the hook is answered at once, and the next call of the same turn runs in the new mode. In acceptEdits a hook "ask" still reaches `can_use_tool` (an escalation waits). In plan mode Claude Code wrote its plan with Write to `~/.claude/plans`, outside the project. (2) **Protocol.** Request `permission_mode.set {mode}` (result `{}`), event `permission_mode.changed {mode, client}` to every client, snapshot field `permission_mode` (the mode Claude Code last reported; before its first report the mode calu started it in; null while unknown). (3) **Offered: default, acceptEdits, auto** (`SWITCHABLE_PERMISSION_MODES`). **Refused with a reason each** (`REFUSED_PERMISSION_MODES`): plan (a plan file outside the project, and leaving plan mode is an approval calu does not present as a decision; not trivial, so not built), dontAsk (an escalation would become a denial without asking anybody), bypassPermissions (Claude Code accepts it only for a session started with `--dangerously-skip-permissions`, which calu never does, and it would switch off Claude Code's own approval of what the checkers pass). With `claude.require_auto_mode` every mode but auto is refused, naming the setting. A mode Claude Code reports by itself (plan after EnterPlanMode) is shown as reported, and the switch leads out of it. (4) **Session.** `Session.set_permission_mode`: refuse? -> audit entry (`permission_mode`, `{mode: before}` -> `{mode: new}`, the client) -> runner -> event; a refusal by Claude Code adds a second entry (actor "session: Claude Code refused the change"). It works between turns and inside a turn, from the next tool call on (a call whose hook has started keeps its mode); the mode becomes `claude.permission_mode` of the process, so a resume or fork keeps it, and the project's settings are unchanged. The runner reports the main agent's mode (`PermissionModeChanged` from init, status and hook inputs); the session passes a change on once, naming the client whose request it answers. (5) **Relay (D242).** A server-token client may switch the mode like any client: `permission_mode.set` is not read-only, so the relay checks the server token again before it, and the session checks its own token. (6) **Clients.** VSCode and PyCharm: a "mode" selector next to the supervision level and `/mode`; web live view: a select (in `LIVE_METHODS`); full-screen client: `/mode` with Tab completion of the mode, `mode <name>` in the status line, a notice for a change elsewhere, a row on `/state`; line client: `/mode [mode]`, the mode on `/state`, the event printed (also by `calu run`); ACP: the session modes stay the supervision level, so the permission mode is a select config option `claude_permission_mode` (category `_claude_permission_mode`), present once a mode is known. **Not done:** plan mode; a real-CLI check of the whole path (TESTING.md manual item 43). |
| D248 | **The final acceptance round's answers (test plan J): one usage panel, side questions in the web, Claude Code's slash commands in the full-screen client, outdated shipped instructions, `~` in session lists** (2026-11-19, the owner's answers after round J, and his later rename of `/context`). (1) **Usage panel.** Request `usage.panel` -> `UsagePanelResult`: the limits calu stored from Claude Code's `rate_limit_event` in this process, each with `reported_at` (new field of `RateLimit`; "not reported yet by Claude Code" when none), the plan's limits Claude Code reads now (control request `get_usage`, which Claude Code marks experimental: utilisation 0-100 per window, `subscription_type`), Claude Code's `/usage` text (control request `get_session_cost`, "the same text /usage prints in non-interactive mode"), cost, turns, context. Measured (`spikes/spike_usage_control.py`, CLI 2.1.319): both requests answer without a prompt, send nothing to the model and start no turn, so the panel records nothing and the checkers see nothing (`/usage` typed as chat text stays a turn of its own, as J-U1 found). `ClaudeRunner.usage_report`; read-only and a control method of the server. Clients: the full-screen client opens it with `/usage` and a click on the cost, context or limit cell of the status line (style meta `cell: usage`, `StatusLine`), the line client with `/usage`, the web live view with a click on the usage line ("usage panel", a dialog). The limits are kept per calu process, not in the database. (2) **Web side questions.** `side.ask` and `usage.panel` are in `LIVE_METHODS`; the relay answers both from a task of their own, so that an Allow is not held up behind a side question. The answers and their history (sent as `history`, at most 20) stay in that page's memory. (3) **Slash commands.** The full-screen client and the line client send every `/word` that is not theirs to Claude as chat text, as VSCode and PyCharm do; the completion list adds `/compact`, `/clear`, `/context`, `/cost`, marked "Claude Code:". calu's own `/context` (the conversation as the checkers read it) is renamed `/checker-context` in both terminal clients (the owner), so no double-slash form is needed. The one name left on both sides is `/usage`: calu's usage panel, which contains Claude Code's `/usage` text, so nothing is lost. The refusal of `/cost` is gone. (4) **Outdated shipped instructions.** `defaults/checkers/shipped.json` holds per editable text (the checker types, `honesty-rules`, `trace-redaction`) the SHA-256 of every version calu ever shipped, with and without its test triggers, written by `scripts/shipped_instructions.py` from the git history; `tests/unit/test_shipped_instructions.py` fails until a changed default is in it. Hashes, not a stored "shipped hash" column: no migration, and a database set up long ago is judged as well. `shipped_status`: current, outdated (an older shipped text, unchanged; the update keeps a removed test-trigger section removed) or edited (never replaced, reported with a diff). `calu setup` asks once on a terminal, replaces without one, keeps with `--no-update-instructions`; `calu doctor --db` notes each; `calu checker show|edit` say it on stderr; new `calu checker diff|update NAME`; the web settings pane shows the sentence, the diff and "Use the present version". For a stored copy of the honesty rules or the redaction instructions the same sentence names `calu honesty reset` / `calu traces instructions reset`. (5) The web's "Recorded turns" leaves out a turn that has not ended. (6) **Paths.** `calu.homes.short_path`: the session user's home as `~` (this machine's user database; `/home/<user>` assumed for another host), another user's of this machine as `~user`; `/running` of both terminal clients and the web's session lists, the full path in a tooltip (web) or `/state`. (7) **Small points of round J.** The line client's `/running` through a relay shows name and time in state, aligned; the full-screen client's `/running` aligns the state column; a reconnecting full-screen client offers no allow, deny or interrupt; the web counts a waiting subagent once; thinking deltas are thinking lines in the web; `--database operator` refuses a blank admin password with an explanation instead of an OperationalError. **Not changed:** a small backward clock jump stays an error; the terminal client follows a restarted session. **Not done:** the editor plugins (usage panel, slash mapping follow later); the TUI's checkers screen does not show the shipped status yet. |
| D249 | **A root client asks the admin password once per run and keeps the narrowest login; each action takes the narrowest role** (2026-11-19, the owner's decision: "asked ONCE per client run, as D214 planned", and "consider multiple DB users with different privileges"; closes the open point of D230). (1) **The defect.** `calu attach --sudo` and `calu start --sudo` held the owner login of root's `admin` entry, but `/running`, `/switch`, `/new`, `/fork`, saving instructions and `/honesty` were given the login of `calu_admin` without a password, so the full-screen client asked the admin password at each of them (unless root's store had a password-free `calu_admin` entry); a client found through a root socket held nothing and asked at each one too. (2) **Now** (`cli._hold_for_client`, before the switch to the user's file identity): the admin password is asked at most once per client run. Where it was typed to open the `admin` entry (the database path), that answer is reused; otherwise (a root socket) the full-screen client asks it once before it starts (Ctrl-D skips; three wrong answers attach without the database features). The client keeps `calu_admin` with that password where the role accepts it (checked by one login) and drops the owner login; with a blank admin password or a schema before migration 0033 it keeps the owner login the password opened. That login serves every later step, passed complete to the client (`StoreAdmin(database=...)`, no dialog) and used for minting and revoking tokens and for `/switch`. It stays in the process's memory: no file, no environment variable, nothing passed to the session or to a program the client starts (checked as root in the root box). The line client asks no admin password. `--no-password` setups are unchanged (the password-free entries come first, nothing is asked); a client that is not root still asks at each change and keeps nothing (D213). (3) **Each action takes the narrowest role** (`calu.db.roles.narrow`, `SET ROLE` after login): listing sessions and servers, naming a session, filing and withdrawing session requests, minting and revoking tokens run as `calu_operator`; checker instructions and settings as `calu_admin`. `session_user` stays the login, so what calu records as the actor (`requested_by`, `resolved_by`) is unchanged, and the audit log's `current_user` names `calu_admin` also for an owner login.
